\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})<0
, where
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}
is a minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{c}
in
X_{T}
given by Lemma
5.7
. By (
5.11
) in Lemma
5.3
, there exists a sequence
(t_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
[T,+\infty)
such that
t_{n}\to+\infty
and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))=0.

(5.174)

Up to subsequences, we have that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})\in\mathscr{F}^{+}_{r_{0}^{+}}
. Hence, we can define

\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{n}(s):=\begin{cases}\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s)&\mbox{ if }s\leq t,\\
(1+t_{n}-s)\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})+(s-t_{n})P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))&\mbox{ if }t_{n}\leq s\leq t_{n}+1,\\
P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))&\mbox{ if }t_{n}+1\leq s.\end{cases}

(5.175)

We have that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}^{n}_{c,T}(s))

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{t_{n}}\mathbf{e}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))ds+\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})-P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}

(5.176)

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n}+1}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}^{n}_{c,T}(s))ds

(5.177)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})+\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})-P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}

(5.178)

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n}+1}\mathcal{E}((1+t_{n}-s)\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})+(s-t_{n})P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})))ds,

(5.179)

where we have used that
t_{n}\geq T
in order to obtain the inequality. Let
\beta^{+}(\hat{r}^{+})
be as in Lemma
5.9
. Up to a subsequence, we have that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))\leq\beta^{+}(\hat{r}^{+})
. Therefore, by Lemma
5.9
we have that for all
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds

\mathcal{E}(\lambda\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})+(1-\lambda)P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})))\leq\mathsf{C}^{+}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})),

(5.180)

where
\mathsf{C}^{+}>0
is independent on
n
(see (
4.34
)). Plugging into (
5.176
), we obtain that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds

\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}^{n}_{c,T}(s))\leq\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})+\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})-P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}+\mathsf{C}^{+}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})).

(5.181)

Recall that we assume Notice also that (
5.12
) implies in particular that

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n})-P^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t_{n}))\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}=0

(5.182)

which, in combination with inequalities (
5.174
) and (
5.181
) together with the fact that
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})<0
, gives that there exists
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{N})<0
. Since
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{N}
is constant in
[t_{N}+1,+\infty)
, we have that for all
\tilde{c}>0
,
\mathbf{E}_{\tilde{c}}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{N})<+\infty
. Therefore, by Lemma
5.5
we have that

\tilde{c}\in(0,+\infty)\to\mathbf{E}_{\tilde{c}}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{N})\in{\mathbb{R}}

(5.183)

is well defined and continuous. Therefore, we can find some
\delta>0
such that for all
\tilde{c}\in(c-\delta,c+\delta)
,
\mathbf{E}_{\tilde{c}}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{N})<0
. As a consequence, we have that
(c-\delta,c+\delta)\subset\mathcal{C}
, which shows that
\mathcal{C}
is open.

We now assume that
(H7’)
holds and we use it to establish the bound (
5.170
). In particular, we can apply Proposition
5.1
. Let
c>0
and
T\geq 1
be such that
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})<0
with
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}\in X_{T}
a minimizing solution given by Lemma
5.7
. Let
t^{\pm}:=t^{\pm}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\pm})
be as in (
5.86
), (
5.87
). Inequality (
5.163
) in Proposition
5.1
implies that
t^{-}<t^{+}
. Recall the definition of
d_{0}
in (
4.36
) and the fact that
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t^{\pm})\in\mathscr{F}^{\pm}_{r_{0}^{\pm}/2}
. Those facts imply

d_{0}\leq\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t^{+})-\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}.

(5.184)

Since
(H7’)
holds, we can use (
5.162
) in Corollary
5.1
to obtain

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t^{+})-\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(t)\rVert^{2}}{2}e^{ct}dt\left(\frac{e^{-ct^{-}}-e^{-ct^{+}}}{c}\right)

(5.185)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})-\frac{a}{c}e^{ct^{-}}\right)\left(\frac{e^{-ct^{-}}-e^{-ct^{+}}}{c}\right).

(5.186)

Using now that
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})\leq 0
, the fact that
t^{-}<t^{+}
and (
5.184
), the inequality above becomes

d_{0}^{2}\leq-2a\frac{1-e^{c(t^{-}-t^{+})}}{c^{2}}\leq\frac{-2a}{c^{2}},

(5.187)

so that (
5.170
) follows.
∎

We now have all the ingredients for establishing the existence of the unconstrained solutions:

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume that
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
,
(H6’)
and
(H7’)
hold. Let
\overline{c}\in\partial(\mathcal{C})\cap(0,+\infty)
, where
\partial(\mathcal{C})
stands for the boundary of the set
\mathcal{C}
defined in (
5.169
). Then, there exists
\overline{T}\geq 1
such that
\mathbf{m}_{\overline{c},\overline{T}}=0
(
\mathbf{m}_{\overline{c},\overline{T}}
as in (
5.15
)) and
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in X_{\overline{T}}
an associated minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}
in
X_{\overline{T}}
which does not saturate the constraints, i. e.

\forall t\geq\overline{T},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{+})<\frac{r_{0}^{+}}{2}

(5.188)

and

\forall t\leq-\overline{T},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{-})<\frac{r_{0}^{-}}{2}.

(5.189)

Moreover,
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})
and the pair
(\overline{c},\overline{\mathbf{U}})
solves (
4.1
).

Remark 5.3
.

Notice that Lemma
5.11
implies that (under the necessary assumptions) the set
\mathcal{C}
is bounded, meaning that
\partial(\mathcal{C})\cap(0,+\infty)\not=\emptyset
. Such a fact, in combination with Proposition
5.2
shows the existence of the unconstrained solutions.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

By Lemma
5.11
, we have that
\mathcal{C}\not=\emptyset
is open, which implies that
\partial(\mathcal{C})\subset{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\mathcal{C}
. Therefore, we have
\overline{c}\not\in\mathcal{C}
. Recall that due to the definition of
\mathcal{C}
in (
5.169
), we have that

\forall T\geq 1,\hskip 5.69054pt\mathbf{m}_{\overline{c},T}\geq 0.

(5.190)

The definition of the boundary allows to consider a sequence
(c_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
contained in
\mathcal{C}
such that
c_{n}\to\overline{c}
. Then, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists
T_{n}\geq 1
such that
\mathbf{E}_{c_{n}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}})<0
, where, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}
is a minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{c_{n}}
in
X_{T_{n}}
. For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, set
t_{n}^{\pm}:=t^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\pm})
as in (
5.86
), (
5.87
). Using (
5.163
) in Corollary
5.1
we have that that

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\hskip 5.69054pt0<t^{+}_{n}-t^{-}_{n}\leq\mathsf{T}_{\star}(c_{n}),

(5.191)

and the function

c\in(0,+\infty)\to\mathsf{T}_{\star}(c)\in(0,+\infty)

(5.192)

is continuous. Since the sequence
(c_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is bounded, we have that

\mathsf{T}_{\star}:=\max\left\{1,\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\mathsf{T}_{\star}(c_{n})\right\}<+\infty

(5.193)

and

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\hskip 5.69054pt0<t^{+}_{n}-t^{-}_{n}\leq\mathsf{T}_{\star},

(5.194)

so that we have a bound on
(t^{+}_{n}-t^{-}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Moreover, (
5.160
) and (
5.161
) in Corollary
5.1
imply

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\forall t\geq t^{+}_{n},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{+})<\frac{r_{0}^{+}}{2}

(5.195)

and

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\forall t\leq t^{-}_{n},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{-})<\frac{r_{0}^{-}}{2}.

(5.196)

For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define the function
\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}}:=\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}(\cdot+t_{n}^{+})
. Then, (
5.194
) implies that (
5.195
) and (
5.196
) write as

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\forall t\geq 0,\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{-})<\frac{r_{0}^{-}}{2}.

(5.197)

and

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\forall t\leq-\mathsf{T}_{\star},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{-})<\frac{r_{0}^{-}}{2}.

(5.198)

so that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
we have
\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\in X_{\mathsf{T}_{\star}}
. Moreover, a computation shows

\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathbf{E}_{c_{n}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}})=e^{-c_{n}t_{n}^{+}}\mathbf{E}_{c_{n}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}})<0.

(5.199)

Therefore, if we apply Lemma
5.6
with sequence of speeds
(c_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and the sequence
(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
X_{\mathsf{T}_{\star}}
, we obtain
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in X_{\mathsf{T}_{\star}}
such that

\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{E}_{c_{n}}(\mathbf{U}_{c_{n},T_{n}}^{t_{n}^{+}})\leq 0,

(5.200)

which in combination with (
5.190
) implies that
\mathbf{m}_{\overline{c},\mathsf{T}_{\star}}=0
. Therefore, we have
\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})=0
, so that
\overline{\mathbf{U}}
is a minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}
in
X_{\mathsf{T}_{\star}}
. Set
t^{\pm}_{\star}:=t^{\pm}(\overline{\mathbf{U}},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{\pm})
as in (
5.86
), (
5.87
). Invoking (
5.190
) and Corollary
5.2
, we obtain that for all
T\geq 1
such that
\mathbf{m}_{\overline{c},T}=0
and
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in X_{T}
, we have
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\overline{c},T})
with

S_{\overline{c},T}:=(-\infty,t^{-}_{\star}+\delta_{\star}(T))\cup(-T,T)\cup(t^{+}_{\star}-\delta_{\star}(T),+\infty)

(5.201)

for some
\delta_{\star}(T)>0
and

\overline{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime\prime}-D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})=-\overline{c}\overline{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\mbox{ in }S_{\overline{c},T}.

(5.202)

Moreover, using (
5.160
) and (
5.161
) in Corollary
5.1
, we obtain as before that

\forall t\geq t^{+}_{\star},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{+})<\frac{r_{0}^{+}}{2}

(5.203)

and

\forall t\leq t^{-}_{\star},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}}(t),\mathscr{F}^{-})<\frac{r_{0}^{-}}{2}.

(5.204)

Therefore, if we set
\overline{T}=\max\{1,t^{+}_{\star},-t^{-}_{\star}\}
, then (
5.203
) and (
5.204
) imply that
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in X_{\overline{T}}
and that (
5.188
), (
5.189
) hold. Moreover, we have that
\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})=0
, so that
\overline{\mathbf{U}}
is a minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}
in
X_{\overline{T}}
by (
5.190
). Therefore, we obtain that
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathcal{A}(S_{\overline{c},\overline{T}})
and

\overline{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime\prime}-D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})=-\overline{c}\overline{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\mbox{ in }S_{\overline{c},\overline{T}},

(5.205)

with
S_{\overline{c},\overline{T}}
as in (
5.201
). The choice of
\overline{T}
implies that
S_{\overline{c},\overline{T}}={\mathbb{R}}
. Therefore,
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})
and
(\overline{c},\overline{\mathbf{U}})
solves (
4.1
), which finishes the proof.
∎

Notice that our Proposition
5.2
follows very similar lines than the analogous results in
[
6
]
and
[
7
]
.

5.8
Uniqueness of the speed

The precise statement of the uniqueness result is as follows:

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume that
(H6’)
holds. Let
X
be the set defined in (
4.39
). Let
(c_{1},c_{2})\in(0,+\infty)^{2}
be such that there exist
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
in
X\cap\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})
such that
(c_{1},\mathbf{U}_{1})
and
(c_{2},\mathbf{U}_{2})
solve (
4.1
) and for each
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathbf{E}_{c_{i}}(\mathbf{U}_{i})<+\infty
. Assume moreover that

\forall i\in\{1,2\},\forall j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathbf{E}_{c_{i}}(\mathbf{U}_{j})\geq 0.

(5.206)

Then, we have
c_{1}=c_{2}
.

Proof.

We prove the result by contradiction. Hence, we can assume without loss of generality that
c_{1}<c_{2}
. A direct computation shows that for every
(c,U)\in(0,+\infty)\times(X\cap\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}}))
a solution to (
4.1
), we have

\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{\lVert U^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{E}(U(t))=e^{-ct}\left(\frac{e^{ct}}{c}\left(\mathcal{E}(U(t))-\frac{\lVert U^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)\right)^{\prime}.

(5.207)

Replacing
(c_{2},U_{2})
in (
5.207
) and multiplying for each
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
by
e^{c_{1}t}
, computations show that

\displaystyle\forall t_{1}<t_{2},\hskip 5.69054ptc_{1}\mathbf{E}_{c_{1}}(\mathbf{U}_{2};(t_{1},t_{2}))

\displaystyle=(c_{1}-c_{2})\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}e^{c_{1}t}dt

(5.208)

\displaystyle+\left[e^{c_{1}t}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(t))-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)\right]^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}.

(5.209)

Notice now that the definition of
X
in (
4.39
) implies that

X=\bigcup_{T\geq 1}X_{T},

(5.210)

which means that there exists
T\geq 1
such that
\mathbf{U}_{2}\in X_{T}
. Combining then Lemma
5.1
and the fact that
\mathbf{E}_{c_{2}}(\mathbf{U}_{2})<+\infty
, we get that
\mathbf{e}_{c_{2}}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(\cdot))\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
. Therefore, we can find two sequences
(t_{n}^{+})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
(t_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
such that
t_{n}^{\pm}\to\pm\infty
and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{e}_{c_{2}}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(t_{n}^{\pm}))=0.

(5.211)

Since we have
c_{1}<c_{2}
, it holds

\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 5.69054pte^{c_{1}t}\left\lvert\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(t))-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right\rvert\leq\lvert\mathbf{e}_{c_{2}}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(t))\rvert

(5.212)

which in combination with (
5.211
) implies

\lim_{n\to\infty}e^{c_{1}t_{n}^{\pm}}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{2}(t_{n}^{\pm}))-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{\pm})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)=0.

(5.213)

Therefore, if we replace for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
in (
5.208
) with
a=t_{n}^{-}
and
b=t_{n}^{+}
, we can then pass to the limit (
5.213
) and obtain

c_{1}\mathbf{E}_{c_{1}}(\mathbf{U}_{2})=(c_{1}-c_{2})\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}e^{c_{1}t}dt<0

(5.214)

because we assume
c_{1}<c_{2}
. However, by (
5.206
) we have
\mathbf{E}_{c_{1}}(\mathbf{U}_{2})\geq 0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Again, the proof of Proposition
5.3
is essentially a direct adaptation of that given in
[
6
]
and
[
7
]
. Our hypothesis are slightly weaker, since we only assume that the solutions have finite energies and (
5.206
), while in
[
6
]
and
[
7
]
it is assumed that the solutions are global minimizers of the corresponding energy functionals. Notice also that
(H7’)
is not needed for proving Proposition
5.3
, which holds in a more general setting.

Proposition
5.3
along with Proposition
5.2
allows to show that the set
\mathcal{C}
defined in (
5.169
) is in fact an open interval:

Corollary 5.3
.

Assume that
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
,
(H6’)
and
(H7’)
hold. Let

c(\mathcal{C}):=\sup\mathcal{C}.

(5.215)

Then, we have
\mathcal{C}=(0,c(\mathcal{C}))
.

Proof.

The statement of the result is equivalent to show that

\partial(\mathcal{C})\cap(0,+\infty)=\{c(\mathcal{C})\}

(5.216)

The quantity
c(\mathcal{C})
is well defined in
(0,+\infty)
because
\mathcal{C}
is non-empty and bounded by Lemma
5.11
. Therefore, we have
c(\mathcal{C})\in\partial(\mathcal{C})\cap(0,+\infty)
because
\mathcal{C}
is open, so it does not contain its limit points. By Proposition
5.2
, we find
\mathbf{U}^{\mathcal{C}}\in X
such that
(c(\mathcal{C}),\mathbf{U}^{\mathcal{C}})
solves (
4.1
). Let now
\overline{c}\in\partial(\mathcal{C})\cap(0,+\infty)
. If we show that
\overline{c}=c(\mathcal{C})
, the proof will be finished. Applying Proposition
5.2
with
\overline{c}
, we find
\overline{\mathbf{U}}\in X
such that
(\overline{c},\overline{\mathbf{U}})
solves (
4.1
). Proposition
5.2
, along with the fact that
\overline{c}
and
c(\mathcal{C})
do not belong to
\mathcal{C}
, also implies that

\inf_{U\in X}\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(U)=\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})=0=\mathbf{E}_{c(\mathcal{C})}(\mathbf{U}^{\mathcal{C}})=\inf_{U\in X}\mathbf{E}_{c(\mathcal{C})}(U)

(5.217)

so that

\mathbf{E}_{c(\mathcal{C})}(\overline{\mathbf{U}})\geq 0\mbox{ and }\mathbf{E}_{\overline{c}}(\mathbf{U}^{\mathcal{C}})\geq 0

(5.218)

meaning that we can apply Proposition
5.3
to
(c(\mathcal{C}),\mathbf{U}^{\mathcal{C}})
,
(\overline{c},\overline{\mathbf{U}})
. As a consequence, we have
\overline{c}=c(\mathcal{C})
, which concludes the proof.
∎

5.9
Proof of Theorem
5
completed

All the elements of the proof of Theorem
5
are already present in previous result. Indeed, Proposition
5.2
along with Corollary
5.3
implies the existence of
(c^{\star},\mathbf{U})\in(0,+\infty)\times X_{T^{\star}}
a solution to (
4.1
) with
c^{\star}=c(\mathcal{C})
. Conditions (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) are satisfied due to the fact that
\mathbf{U}\in X_{T^{\star}}
. The statement regarding the uniqueness of the speed
c^{\star}
follows from Proposition
5.3
. Finally, we have that (
5.12
) is exactly the exponential rate of convergence (
4.42
), which completes the proof.

∎

5.10
Asymptotic behavior of the constrained solutions at
-\infty

As it has been pointed out before, almost nothing can be said about the behavior of arbitrary function in
X_{T}
at
-\infty
. However, it turns out that constrained minimizers converge exponentially at
-\infty
with respect to the
\mathscr{L}
-norm provided that the speed fulfills an explicit upper bound, see Proposition
5.4
. Such an upper bound also allows to establish some other properties. Once Proposition
5.4
will have been established, we will be able to complete the proofs of Theorems
6
and
7
. The results of this subsection are obtained by combining ideas from Smyrnelis
[
69
]
, Alikakos and Katzourakis
[
7
]
, Alikakos, Fusco and Smyrnelis
[
6
]
. It is worth to point out that the arguments we present here strongly rely on the fact that the solutions considered are minimizers and that we do not expect them to hold for more general critical points.

We begin by showing the following preliminary result, which follows by a direct computation:

Lemma 5.12
.

Assume that
(H6’)
holds. Let
c>0
,
t_{1}<t_{2}
and
U\in\mathcal{A}((t_{1},t_{2}))
such that

U^{\prime\prime}-D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(U)=-cU^{\prime}\mbox{ in }(t_{1},t_{2}).

(5.219)

Then, we have the formula

\forall t\in(t_{1},t_{2}),\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{E}(U(t))-\frac{\lVert U^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)=c\lVert U^{\prime}(t)\rVert^{2}_{\mathscr{L}}.

(5.220)

Lemma
5.12
gives the following pointwise bounds for constrained solutions:

Lemma 5.13
.

Assume that
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
,
(H6’)
and
(H7’)
hold. Let
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}
be a constrained solution given by Lemma
5.7
and
t^{-}:=t^{-}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{-})
be as in (
5.86
). Then for all
t<t^{-}
we have the inequality

\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\leq\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t))-a.

(5.221)

Similarly, it holds that for all
t>t^{+}

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t))\leq\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2},

(5.222)

where
t^{+}:=t^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{+})
is as in (
5.87
).

Proof.

Notice that (
5.168
) in Corollary
5.2
implies that
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}
solves

\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime\prime}-D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})=-c\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}\mbox{ in }(-\infty,t^{-}).

(5.223)

Therefore, the function

f_{c,T}:t\in(-\infty,t^{-}]\to e^{ct}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t))-a-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right),

(5.224)

is
C^{1}
and we clearly have that
f_{c,T}\in L^{1}((-\infty,t^{-}])
. By (
5.220
) in Lemma
5.12
, we have

\forall t\in(-\infty,t^{-}),\hskip 5.69054ptf_{c,T}^{\prime}(t)=cf_{c,T}(t)+ce^{ct}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}_{c,T}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}\geq 0,

(5.225)

and we also have
f_{c,T}^{\prime}\in L^{1}((-\infty,t^{-}))
. Therefore, it holds that

\lim_{t\to-\infty}f_{c,T}(t)=0.

(5.226)

Fix
t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t^{-}
. Integrating (
5.225
) in
[t_{1},t_{2}]
we get

f_{c,T}(t_{2})\geq f_{c,T}(t_{1}),

(5.227)

which in combination with (
5.226
) gives

\forall t<t^{-},\hskip 5.69054ptf_{c,T}(t)\geq 0,

(5.228)

which is (
5.221
). Inequality (
5.222
) is obtained in an identical fashion.
∎

We conclude this subsection by proving the exponential convergence result, which is inspired by the ideas in
Proof of (28)
in Smyrnelis
[
69
]
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Assume that
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
,
(H6’)
and
(H7’)
hold. Let
c>0
and
T\geq 1
. Assume moreover that
c<\gamma^{-}
, where
\gamma^{-}
is defined in (
4.35
). Let
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}
be a constrained solution given by Lemma
5.7
. Then, there exists
\overline{M}^{-}>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma^{-}-c)
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
it holds

\int_{-\infty}^{t}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))-a)e^{-\varepsilon s}ds\leq\overline{M}^{-}e^{(\gamma^{-}-c-\varepsilon)t}.

(5.229)

Furthermore, there exist
M^{-}>0
and
\mathbf{v}_{c,T}^{-}\in\mathscr{F}^{-}
such that for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}

\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-\mathbf{v}_{c,T}^{-}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}\leq M^{-}e^{(\gamma^{-}-c)t}.

(5.230)

Proof.

Let
t^{-}:=t^{-}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T},\mathcal{E}_{\max}^{-})
be as in (
5.86
). By applying (
5.160
) in Corollary
5.1
, we obtain that for all
t\leq t^{-}
,
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)\in\mathscr{F}^{-}_{r_{0}^{-}/2}
. For all
t\leq t^{-}
, define
\mathbf{v}^{-}(t):=P^{-}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t))
. Consider the function

\tilde{U}^{-}_{t}(s):=\begin{cases}\mathbf{v}^{-}(t)&\mbox{ if }s\leq t-1,\\
(t-s)\mathbf{v}^{-}(t)+(s-t+1)\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)&\mbox{ if }t-1\leq s\leq t,\\
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s)&\mbox{ if }t\leq s,\end{cases}

(5.231)

which belongs to
X_{T}
. Therefore,

\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T})\leq\mathbf{E}_{c}(\tilde{U}_{t}^{-})

(5.232)

and, equivalently

\int_{-\infty}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))ds\leq\frac{a}{c}e^{ct}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\left(\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-\mathbf{v}^{-}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}+(\mathcal{E}(\tilde{U}^{-}_{t}(s))-a)\right)e^{cs}ds.

(5.233)

Using Lemma
5.9
and (
4.7
) in
(H1’)
, (
5.233
) becomes

\int_{-\infty}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))ds\leq\frac{a}{c}e^{ct}+(C^{-}+\mathsf{C}^{-})(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-a)e^{ct},

(5.234)

which gives

\int_{-\infty}^{t}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s)-a)e^{cs}ds\leq\frac{1}{\gamma^{-}}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-a)e^{ct},

(5.235)

where
\gamma^{-}
was defined in (
4.35
). Define the function

\theta^{-}_{c,T}:t\in(-\infty,t^{-}]\to\int_{-\infty}^{t}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s)-a)e^{cs}ds\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(5.236)

By
(H6’)
, the function
\theta_{c,T}^{-}
defined in (
5.236
) verifies that for all
t\in(-\infty,t^{-})

(\theta^{-}_{c,T})^{\prime}(t)=(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t))-a)e^{ct}

(5.237)

which, by (
5.235
) implies

\forall t\leq t^{-},\hskip 5.69054pt\gamma^{-}\theta_{c,T}^{-}(t)\leq(\theta^{-}_{c,T})^{\prime}(t).

(5.238)

Fix now
t\in(-\infty,t^{-})
and assume that
\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)>0
. The previous inequality is equivalent to

\gamma^{-}\leq(\ln(\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)))^{\prime}.

(5.239)

which, by integrating in
[t,t^{-}]
becomes

\gamma^{-}(t^{-}-t)\leq\ln(\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-}))-\ln(\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)),

(5.240)

hence

e^{\gamma^{-}(t^{-}-t)}\leq\frac{\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-})}{\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)},

(5.241)

that is

\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)e^{\gamma^{-}(t^{-}-t)}\leq\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-}),

(5.242)

which clearly also holds if we drop the assumption
\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)>0
, as
\theta^{-}_{c,T}
is a non-negative function. Thus, we have shown that

\forall t\leq t^{-},\hskip 5.69054pt\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t)\leq\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-})e^{-\gamma^{-}(t^{-}-t)}.

(5.243)

Now, we have that using (
5.243
) we get for any fixed
t\leq t^{-}-1
,
\varepsilon>0
and
i\in{\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\int_{t-i-1}^{t-i}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))-a)e^{-\varepsilon s}ds

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(c+\varepsilon)(t-i-1)}\int_{t-i-1}^{t-i}(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(s))-a)e^{cs}ds

(5.244)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(c+\varepsilon)(t-i-1)}\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-})e^{-\gamma^{-}(t^{-}-t+i)}

(5.245)

\displaystyle=e^{(c+\varepsilon)(1-t^{-})}\theta^{-}_{c,T}(t^{-})e^{(\gamma^{-}-c-\varepsilon)(t-t^{-})}e^{(c+\varepsilon-\gamma^{-})i}.

(5.246)

Since we assume that
c<\gamma^{-}
, we have that by choosing any
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma^{-}-c)
it holds

\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}e^{(c+\varepsilon-\gamma^{-})i}=\frac{1}{1-e^{(c+\varepsilon-\gamma^{-})}},

(5.247)

which, in combination with (
5.244
) gives (
5.229
) (notice that the case
t>t^{-}-1
presents no problem, as
e^{(\gamma^{-}-c-\varepsilon)t}
is then large). Therefore, by (
5.221
) in Lemma
5.13
we have that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma^{-}-c)
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}

\int_{-\infty}^{t}\frac{\lVert U^{\prime}(s)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}e^{-\varepsilon s}ds\leq\overline{M}^{-}e^{(\gamma^{-}-c-\varepsilon)t},

(5.248)

which, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, means that

\int_{-\infty}^{t}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(s)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}ds\leq\left(\frac{e^{\varepsilon t}}{\varepsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}(s)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}e^{-\varepsilon s}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{2\overline{M}^{-}}{\varepsilon}e^{(\gamma^{-}-c)t},

(5.249)

where we have used that
\lim_{s\to-\infty}e^{\varepsilon s}=0
, because
\varepsilon>0
. Since
c<\gamma^{-}
, in particular inequality (
5.249
) implies the existence of some
\tilde{v}^{-}\in\mathscr{L}
such that

\lim_{t\to-\infty}\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-\tilde{v}^{-}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}=0.

(5.250)

Inequality (
5.249
) also implies that for all
\tilde{t}<t\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\lVert\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(t)-\mathbf{U}_{c,T}(\tilde{t}^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}\leq\frac{2\overline{M}^{-}}{\varepsilon}e^{(\gamma^{-}-c)t},

(5.251)

which by taking the limit
\tilde{t}\to-\infty
and using (
5.250
) gives (
5.230
), by choosing for instance
\varepsilon=(\gamma^{-}-c)/2\in(0,\gamma^{-}-c)
and
M^{-}=\frac{2\overline{M}^{-}}{\varepsilon}>0
.

∎

Remark 5.5
.

Notice that combining (
5.229
) in Proposition
5.4
with (
5.221
) in Lemma
5.13
, we obtain in particular that
\mathbf{U}_{c,T}^{\prime}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},\mathscr{L})
provided that
c<\gamma^{-}
(see the statements of the results for the notations).

5.11
Proof of Theorem
6
completed

Assume first that
(H7’)
holds. Let
(c^{\star},\mathbf{U})
be the solution to (
4.1
) with conditions at infinity (
4.2
) and (
4.42
) given by Proposition
5.2
and Lemma
5.3
. Since we took
c^{\star}=\sup\mathcal{C}
with
\mathcal{C}
as in (
5.169
), inequality (
5.170
) in Lemma
5.11
implies that

c^{\star}\leq\frac{\sqrt{-2a}}{d_{0}}

(5.252)

which by (
4.44
) in
(H8’)
implies that

c^{\star}<\gamma^{-}

(5.253)

so that we can apply (
5.230
) in Proposition
5.4
to
\mathbf{U}
, as it is a minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{c^{\star}}
in
X_{T^{\star}}
for some
T^{\star}\geq 1
. Therefore, (
4.45
) holds for
\mathbf{U}
, which completes the proof.

∎

5.12
Proof of Theorem
7
completed

Since we assume that
(H8’)
holds and
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}
is such that
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\in X_{T}
for some
T\geq 1
and
\mathbf{E}_{c^{\star}}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})=0
, then by Proposition
5.2
, we can apply Proposition
5.4
to
\mathbf{U}
. We recall that by Remark
5.5
we have that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},\mathscr{L})
and by (
5.229
) in Proposition
5.4
we have that
\mathcal{E}\circ\mathbf{U}\in L^{1}((-\infty,t])
for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Therefore, we can find a sequence
(t_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}^{-}=-\infty

(5.254)

and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{-}))-a-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)=0.

(5.255)

Similarly, since
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\mathbf{U})=0<+\infty
, we have that
\mathcal{E}\circ\mathbf{U}\in L^{1}([t,+\infty))
for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, which means that we can find
(t_{n}^{+})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
a sequence of real numbers such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}^{+}=+\infty

(5.256)

and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left(\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{+})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}-\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{+}))\right)=0.

(5.257)

Taking the scalar product in
\mathscr{L}
between equation (
4.1
) and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 5.69054pt\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}-\langle D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t)),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}=-c\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}

(5.258)

so that

\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 5.69054pt\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}-(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t)))^{\prime}=-c^{\star}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}.

(5.259)

Fix
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Integrating above in
[t_{n}^{-},t_{n}^{+}]
(which is non-empty up to an extraction) we obtain

\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}dt-\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{+}))+\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{-}))=-c^{\star}\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}dt.

(5.260)

Integrating by parts we obtain

\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}dt=\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{+})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}-\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}-\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}dt,

(5.261)

which means

\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\langle\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}(t),\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{\mathscr{L}}dt=\frac{1}{2}(\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{+})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}-\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}).

(5.262)

Plugging into (
5.260
) we obtain

a+\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{-}))-a-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{-})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}\right)+\left(\frac{\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{+})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}}{2}-\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U}(t_{n}^{+}))\right)=-c^{\star}\int_{t_{n}^{-}}^{t_{n}^{+}}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}dt.

(5.263)

Using (
5.254
), (
5.255
), (
5.256
) and (
5.257
), along with the fact that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},\mathscr{L})
, we can pass to the limit
n\to\infty
and we get that

a=-c^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(t)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}dt,

(5.264)

which shows (
4.47
). We now show that (
4.48
) holds. Inspecting again the proof of Theorem
5
, we have that
c^{\star}
is equal to
c(\mathcal{C})
as in Corollary
5.3
. Take
c<c^{\star}
, then by Corollary
5.3
we have that
c\in\mathcal{C}
. The definitions of
\mathcal{C}
in (
5.169
) implies then that

\exists T\geq 1,\hskip 5.69054pt\inf_{U\in X_{T}}\mathbf{E}_{c}(U)<0

(5.265)

which, by considering
\tilde{U}\in X_{T}
such that
\mathbf{E}_{c}(\tilde{U})<0
and then the sequence
(\tilde{U}(\cdot+n))_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
which is contained in
X
, implies that
\inf_{U\in X}\mathbf{E}_{c}(U)=-\infty
. If we now take
c>c^{\star}
, we have again by Corollary
5.3
that

\forall T\geq 1,\hskip 5.69054pt\inf_{U\in X_{T}}\mathbf{E}_{c}(U)\geq 0

(5.266)

which means

\inf_{U\in X}\mathbf{E}_{c}(U)=0.

(5.267)

Therefore, (
4.48
) follows. Finally, we have that (
4.49
) is exactly (
5.170
) in Lemma
5.11
.
∎

6
Proofs of the main results completed

Once we have proved the abstract results, we are ready to prove the main ones. In order to do such a thing, we need to show that the main problem can be put into the abstract framework. This is shown in Lemma
6.1
which is in subsection
6.1
. The next subsections are then devoted to the conclusion of the proofs of the main results, which are Theorems
1
,
2
and
4
. However, as pointed out before, we do not have a counterpart of Theorem
3
in the abstract setting, which means that we prove it using arguments relative to the main setting.

6.1
Proving the link between the main setting and the abstract setting

The following result establishes the link between the main assumptions and the abstract ones. As a consequence, the main results can be deduced from the abstract framework, which we have already established.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that
(H5)
holds. Set

\mathscr{L}:=L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}),\hskip 5.69054pt\mathscr{H}:=H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}),\hskip 5.69054pt\tilde{\mathscr{H}}:=H^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}),

(6.1)

r_{0}^{\pm}:=\rho_{0}^{\pm}

(6.2)

and

\forall v\in\mathscr{L},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathcal{E}(v):=\begin{cases}E(\psi+v)-\mathfrak{m}^{+}&\mbox{ if }v\in\mathscr{H},\\
+\infty&\mbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

(6.3)

where
\mathfrak{m}^{+}
was introduced in
(H5)
, the constants
\rho_{0}^{\pm}
are those from (
2.9
) and the function
\psi
is any smooth function in
X(\sigma^{-},\sigma^{+})
converging to
\sigma^{\pm}
at
\pm\infty
at an exponential rate and such that
\psi^{\prime}\in H^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. Under this choice, assumptions
(H1’)
,
(H2’)
,
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
and
(H6’)
hold. Moreover, we have:

•

If
(H6)
holds, then
(H7’)
holds.

•

If
(H7)
holds, then
(H8’)
holds.

Proof.

The fact that the functional

v\in\mathscr{H}\to E(\psi+v)

(6.4)

is well defined and, moreover, is a
C^{1}
functional on
(\mathscr{H},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathscr{H}})
is proven by classical arguments. See for instance Bisgard
[
28
]
, Montecchiari and Rabinowitz
[
56
]
. See also
[
60
]
for the precise statement in this setting. We obviously have
\mathscr{F}^{\pm}=\mathcal{F}^{\pm}-\psi
. We now pass to prove that the assumptions are satisfied.

Assumption
(H1’)
is satisfied
:

The fact that
\mathcal{E}
is weakly lower semicontinuous in
\mathscr{L}
is standard, see
Lemma 3.1
in
[
69
]
. We already invoked
Lemma 2.1
in
[
67
]
in Schatzmann so that (
2.9
) and (
2.11
) hold. That is, due to (
6.2
) we have that if

\inf_{\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}}\lVert v+\psi-\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}\leq r_{0}^{\pm}

(6.5)

there is a unique
\tau(v)\in{\mathbb{R}}
which attains the infimum in (
6.5
). Moreover, the correspondence
v\to\tau(v)
defined on the subset of
\mathscr{L}
composed of functions that verify (
6.5
) is of class
C^{2}
. Therefore, the applications

P^{\pm}:v\in\mathscr{F}^{-}_{r_{0}^{\pm}/2}\to\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau(v))-\psi\in\mathscr{F}^{\pm},

(6.6)

satisfy the properties required. Finally, we have that estimate (
4.7
) follows by
Lemma 3.2
in
[
57
]
, up to modifying the choice of the constants
\rho_{0}^{\pm},\beta_{0}^{\pm}
.

Assumption
(H2’)
is satisfied
:

By (
6.1
), we have that
\tilde{\mathscr{H}}\subset\mathscr{H}\subset\mathscr{L}
and the associated norms verify

\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}\leq\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathscr{H}}\leq\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\tilde{\mathscr{H}}}.

(6.7)

As we pointed out before,
\mathcal{E}
restricted to
(\mathscr{H},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathscr{H}})
is a
C^{1}
functional. Moreover, as shown in
[
28
,
56
]
, we have that the differential is given by

\forall v\in\mathscr{H},\hskip 5.69054ptD\mathcal{E}(v):w\in\mathscr{H}\to\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\langle\psi^{\prime}+v^{\prime},w^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\nabla V(\psi+v),w\rangle\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(6.8)

Let now
v\in\tilde{\mathscr{H}}
, since
\psi
is smooth with good behavior at infinity we can integrate by parts to get

\displaystyle\forall w\in\mathscr{H},\hskip 5.69054ptD\mathcal{E}(v)(w)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle-(\psi^{\prime\prime}+v^{\prime\prime})+\nabla V(\psi+v),w\rangle

(6.9)

\displaystyle=\langle D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(v),w\rangle_{\mathscr{L}},

(6.10)

where we have set

D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}:v\in(\tilde{\mathscr{H}},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\tilde{\mathscr{H}}})\to-(\psi^{\prime\prime}+v^{\prime\prime})+\nabla V(\psi+v)\in(\mathscr{L},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}),

(6.11)

which, by standard arguments, can be shown to be continuous. Notice that (
4.13
) in
(H2’)
is exactly (
6.9
) above, which concludes this part of the proof.

Assumption
(H3’)
is satisfied
:

Let
(\mathbf{v}_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a
\mathscr{L}
-bounded sequence in
\mathscr{F}^{-}
. We want to show the existence of a subsequence of
(\mathbf{v}_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
strongly convergent in
\mathscr{H}
. Since

\mathscr{F}^{-}=\mathcal{F}^{-}-\psi=\{\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi:\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}\},

(6.12)

we have that
(\mathbf{v}_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}=(\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau_{n})-\psi)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
with
(\tau_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
a bounded sequence of real numbers. Since such a sequence is bounded in
\mathscr{L}
, we know that, up to an extraction, there exists
\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{L}
such that
\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau_{n})-\psi\rightharpoonup\tilde{v}
weakly in
\mathscr{L}
. Due to the weak lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{E}
, we have that

\mathcal{E}(\tilde{v})\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau_{n})-\psi)=a,

(6.13)

which, by minimality, implies that
\mathcal{E}(v)=0
, that is,
\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{F}^{-}
. We can then write
\tilde{v}=\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi
for some
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Now, notice that, by the compactness of minimizing sequences (
2.6
), there exists a sequence
(\tau_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of real numbers such that, up to an extraction

\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau_{n}+\tau_{n}^{\prime})-\mathfrak{q}^{-}\to 0\mbox{ strongly in }\mathscr{H}

(6.14)

which necessarily implies that

\tau_{n}+\tau_{n}^{\prime}\to 0

(6.15)

and, since
(\tau_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is bounded, we have that
(\tau_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a bounded sequence as well. Therefore, we can assume, up to an extraction, that
\tau_{n}^{\prime}\to\tau
. Combining this information with (
6.14
), we obtain

\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau_{n})-\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot-\tau^{-})\to 0\mbox{ strongly in }\mathscr{H},

(6.16)

which establishes the claim.

We need to show the same for
\mathscr{F}^{+}
. The argument is identical to the one above, except for the fact that the compactness of minimizing sequences is replaced by 3. in assumption
(H5)
, which is in fact stronger, and we use that the elements in
\mathscr{F}^{+}
are local minimizers (instead of global ones), which does not require any modification of the reasoning.

Assumption
(H4’)
is satisfied
:

More precisely, we show that 2. in
(H4’)
holds. Notice that since the results are local in nature and
(H1’)
implies that locally the situation does not change between
\mathscr{F}^{-}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+}
, we can treat both cases together. Let
(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})\in\mathscr{F}^{\pm}_{r_{0}^{\pm}}
. Let
\tau(v)
be given by the projection map defined in (
6.6
). We have that
\mathbf{v}^{\pm}=\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi
for some
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Define

\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}:w\in\mathscr{L}\to w(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)-\psi+\psi(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)\in\mathscr{L}.

(6.17)

Clearly, using the definition of the projection in (
6.6
) and
\tau(v)

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(v)-\mathbf{v}^{\pm}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}

\displaystyle=\lVert v(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)-(\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau))\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}

(6.18)

\displaystyle=\lVert v-(\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau(v))-\psi)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}=\inf_{\tilde{\tau}\in{\mathbb{R}}}\lVert v-(\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tilde{\tau})-\psi)\rVert_{\mathscr{L}},

(6.19)

meaning that

P^{\pm}(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(v))=\mathbf{v}^{\pm}

(6.20)

and

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(v),\mathscr{F}^{\pm})=\mathrm{dist}_{\mathscr{L}}(v,\mathscr{F}^{\pm})

(6.21)

which are (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) respectively. Next, notice that for
(w_{1},w_{2})\in\mathscr{L}^{2}
and
h\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(w_{1}+hw_{2})=\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(w_{1})+hw_{2}(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)

(6.22)

so that
\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}
is differentiable and

\forall(w_{1},w_{2})\in\mathscr{L}^{2},\hskip 5.69054ptD(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})})(w_{1},w_{2})=w_{2}(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)

(6.23)

so that

\forall(w_{1},w_{2})\in\mathscr{L}^{2},\hskip 5.69054pt\lVert D(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})})(w_{1},w_{2})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}=\lVert w_{2}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}},

(6.24)

which is (
4.16
). Finally, notice that
v\in\mathscr{H}
if and only if
\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}\in\mathscr{H}
. Assuming that
v\in\mathscr{H}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(v))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(v(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)-\psi+\psi(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau))

(6.25)

\displaystyle=E(v(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau)+\psi(\cdot-\tau(v)+\tau))=E(\psi+v)=\mathcal{E}(v)

(6.26)

and if
v\in\mathscr{L}\setminus\mathscr{H}
, we have
\mathcal{E}(\hat{P}^{\pm}_{(v,\mathbf{v}^{\pm})}(v))=+\infty=\mathcal{E}(v)
. Therefore, (
4.17
) holds. We have then showed that
(H4’)
holds.

Assumption
(H5’)
is satisfied
:

Lemma
2.1
in Schatzmann states that for
v\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathscr{H},r_{0}^{\pm}}^{\pm}
the problem

\inf_{\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}}\lVert v+\psi-\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau)\rVert_{\mathscr{H}}

(6.27)

has a unique solution
\tau_{\mathscr{H}}(v)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and the projection map

P^{\pm}_{\mathscr{H}}:v\in\mathscr{F}^{\pm}_{\mathscr{H},r_{0}^{\pm}}\to\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}(\cdot+\tau_{\mathscr{H}}(v))\in\mathscr{F}^{\pm}

(6.28)

is
C^{1}
with respect to the
\mathscr{H}
-norm. Next, we have that (
4.20
) is
Corollary 2.3
in
[
67
]
. Finally, the fact that (
4.21
) implies (
4.22
) for the constants
C^{\pm}
(up to possibly increasing) is a consequence of the compactness of the minimizing sequences. See for example
Corollary 3.2
in
[
67
]
.

Assumption
(H6’)
is satisfied
:

We show the existence of the map
\mathfrak{P}
. We follow
Lemma 3.3
in
[
67
]
. Let
R_{0}>0
be the constant from
(H2)
. For
R\geq R_{0}
define in
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}

f_{R}(u):=\begin{cases}u&\mbox{ if }\lvert u\rvert\leq R,\\
R\frac{u}{\lvert u\rvert}&\mbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

(6.29)

where
R_{0}
is the constant from
(H2)
. For
u\in R^{k}
such that
\lvert u\rvert\leq R
, we have
f_{R}(u)=u
. Assume that
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
is such that
\lvert u\rvert>R
. In that case, there exists
\xi\in\left(\frac{R}{\lvert u\rvert},1\right)
such that

V(u)=V(f_{R}(u))+\langle\nabla_{u}V(\xi u),u-f_{R}(u)\rangle=V(f_{R}(u))+\frac{1}{\xi}\left(1-\frac{R}{\lvert u\rvert}\right)\langle\nabla_{u}V(\xi u),\xi u\rangle

(6.30)

which, by
(H2)
implies

\forall R\geq R_{0},\forall u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}:\lvert u\rvert>R,\hskip 5.69054ptV(u)\geq V(f_{R}(u))+\frac{1}{\xi}\left(1-\frac{R}{\lvert u\rvert}\right)\nu_{0}\lvert\xi u\rvert^{2}>V(f_{R}(u)).

(6.31)

In particular, we have shown

\forall R\geq R_{0},\forall u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\hskip 5.69054ptV(u)\geq V(f_{R}(u)).

(6.32)

Next, let
J\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a compact interval and
v\in H^{1}(J,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. For
R\geq R_{0}
, consider the function
v_{R}:=f_{R}\circ v
. Since we clearly have that for all
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
,
\lvert f_{R}(u)\rvert\leq\lvert u\rvert
, it holds that
v_{R}\in L^{2}(J,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. Next, we have that
f_{R}
is the projection onto the closed ball of center 0 and radius
R
, so that it is non-expansive. As a consequence, we have

\forall R\geq R_{0},\forall u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\hskip 5.69054pt\lvert Df_{R}(u)\rvert\leq 1.

(6.33)

Therefore, applying the chain rule we obtain

\mbox{for a. e. }t\in J,\hskip 5.69054pt\lvert v_{R}^{\prime}(t)\rvert\leq\lvert v^{\prime}(t)\rvert,

(6.34)

which means that
v_{R}\in H^{1}(J,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
and, combining with (
6.32
) we obtain

E(v_{R};J)\leq E(v;J)

(6.35)

and, by (
6.31
) the equality above holds if and only if
v_{R}=v
. Let now

R_{\max}:=2\max\{R_{0},\lVert\mathfrak{q}^{-}\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})},\lVert\mathfrak{q}^{+}\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\}.

(6.36)

Consider now the application

\mathfrak{P}:v\in\mathscr{L}\to f_{R_{\max}}\circ(v+\psi)-\psi\in\mathscr{L}

(6.37)

which is well-defined due to the previous considerations. Moreover, the choice of
R_{\max}
implies that
\mathfrak{P}
equals the identity on
\{\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi:\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
and
\{\mathfrak{q}^{+}(\cdot+\tau)-\psi:\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
, which is exactly (
4.28
). Inequality (
6.35
) gives (
4.26
). Finally, using (
6.33
) we have that

\displaystyle\forall(v_{1},v_{2})\in\mathscr{L}^{2},\hskip 5.69054pt\lVert\mathfrak{P}(v_{1})-\mathfrak{P}(v_{2})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lvert f_{R_{\max}}\circ(v_{1}+\psi)-f_{R_{\max}}\circ(v_{2}+\psi)\rvert^{2}

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sup_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}\lvert Df_{R_{\max}}(u)\rvert^{2}\lvert v_{1}-v_{2}\rvert^{2}

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lvert v_{1}-v_{2}\rvert^{2}=\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}}^{2},

(6.40)

which is (
4.27
). Therefore, our map
\mathfrak{P}
satisfies the required properties. Next, let
\mathbf{W}
be a local minimizer of
\mathbf{E}_{c}
. We show that
\mathbf{W}
satisfies the desired regularity properties, that is,
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{A}(I)
with
\mathcal{A}(I)
as in (
4.29
). Write
\overline{\mathbf{W}}:=\mathbf{W}+\psi
. We assume that for all
t\in I
,
\mathbf{W}(t)=\mathfrak{P}(\mathbf{W})
. The definition of
\mathfrak{P}
in (
6.37
) implies that

\forall(x_{1},x_{2})\in I\times{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 5.69054pt\overline{\mathbf{W}}(x_{1},x_{2})=f_{R_{\max}}(\overline{\mathbf{W}}(x_{1},x_{2}))

(6.41)

so that

\lVert\overline{\mathbf{W}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(I\times{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\leq R_{\max}.

(6.42)

Therefore, by classical elliptic regularity arguments, we have that, with the obvious identifications,
\overline{\mathbf{W}}
solves

-c\partial_{x_{1}}\overline{\mathbf{W}}-\Delta\overline{\mathbf{W}}=-\nabla_{u}V(\overline{\mathbf{W}})\mbox{ in }I\times{\mathbb{R}}

(6.43)

and for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
we have that
\overline{\mathbf{W}}\in\mathcal{C}^{3,\alpha}(I_{C}\times{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
for any compact
I_{C}\subset I
. It is then clear that

\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(I_{C},L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}))\cap\mathcal{C}^{1}(I_{C},H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}(I_{C},H^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}))

(6.44)

for any
I_{C}\subset I
compact, which means that
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{A}(I)
.

Assumption
(H6)
implies
(H7’)
: Immediate.

Assumption
(H7)
implies
(H8’)
: Immediate.
∎

Once Lemma
6.1
has been established, the main results are easily obtained by rephrasing the abstract ones.

6.2
Proof of Theorem
1
completed

Assume that
(H6)
holds. Notice that
(H6)
implies that
(H1)
,
(H2)
,
(H3)
,
(H4)
and
(H5)
hold. Therefore, applying Lemma
6.1
we have that, choosing the objects as in its statement, we get that
(H3’)
,
(H4’)
,
(H5’)
,
(H6’)
and
(H7’)
hold. Those are exactly the assumptions which are needed for Theorem
5
to hold, meaning that we obtain
(c^{\star},\mathbf{U})
with
c^{\star}>0
and
\mathbf{U}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})\cap X
, with
\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})
as in (
4.29
) and
X
as in (
4.39
), which solves

\mathbf{U}^{\prime\prime}-D_{\mathscr{L}}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{U})=-c\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mbox{ in }{\mathbb{R}}

(6.45)

and satisfies the conditions at infinity

\exists T^{-}\leq 0:\forall t\leq T^{-},\hskip 5.69054pt\mathbf{U}(t)\in\mathscr{F}^{-}_{r_{0}^{-}/2}\hskip 2.84526pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 2.84526pt\exists\mathbf{v}^{+}(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{F}^{+}:\lim_{t\to+\infty}\lVert\mathbf{U}(t)-\mathbf{v}^{+}(\mathbf{U})\rVert_{\mathscr{H}}=0.

(6.46)

We now pass to prove each of the three statements of Theorem
1
separately:

1.

Existence
. Recall that for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have
\mathbf{U}(t)\in\mathscr{L}=L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. Let us then define

\mathfrak{U}:(x_{1},x_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to\mathbf{U}(x_{1})(x_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}.

(6.47)

It is clear then that since
\mathbf{U}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathbb{R}})
we have that
\mathfrak{U}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
and, moreover for all
(x_{1},x_{2})
and any pair of index
(i,j)\in\{0,1,2\}^{2}
such that
i+j\leq 2
we have

\partial_{x_{1}}^{i}\partial_{x_{2}}^{j}\mathfrak{U}(x_{1},x_{2})=(\mathbf{U}^{(i)}(x_{1}))^{(j)}(x_{2})

(6.48)

where for a curve
f
taking values in a Hilbert space we denote by
f^{(i)}
its
i
-th derivative,
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
. As a consequence of (
6.45
), (
6.48
) and the formula for
D\mathcal{E}
when we make
\mathcal{E}=E-\mathfrak{m}^{+}
(see (
6.8
)) we obtain that

-c\partial_{x_{1}}\mathfrak{U}-\Delta\mathfrak{U}=-\nabla_{u}V(\mathfrak{U})\mbox{ in }{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

(6.49)

and by (
6.46
) we obtain that for some
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have for some
x_{1}\leq L
that
\mathfrak{U}(x_{1},\cdot)\in\mathcal{F}^{-}_{\rho^{-}/2}
, since we choose
r_{0}^{\pm}=\rho^{\pm}
, so that
\mathcal{F}^{\pm}_{\rho^{\pm}/2}=\mathscr{F}^{\pm}_{r_{0}^{\pm}/2}
. The variational characterization (
2.23
) follows directly from Theorem
1
, using the fact that we have
X=S
and
\mathbf{E}_{c}=E_{2,c}
for all
c>0
(again we implicitly identify
\mathfrak{U}
with
\mathbf{U}
via (
6.47
). Finally, we have that for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\mathbf{U}(t)=\mathfrak{P}(\mathbf{U}(t))
. According to the choice of
\mathfrak{P}
made in Lemma
6.1
, this implies that
\lVert\mathfrak{U}\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}<+\infty
, which by classical Schauder theory and the smoothness properties of
V
implies that for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\mathfrak{U}\in\mathcal{C}^{2,\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. The proof of the existence part of Theorem
1
is hence completed.

2.

Uniqueness of the speed
. Again, we have that
X=S
and
\mathbf{E}_{c}=E_{2,c}
for all
c>0
, meaning that the proof of this statement follows from the analogous one in Theorem
5
.

3.

Exponential convergence
. Using the exponential rate of convergence of
\mathbf{U}
given by Theorem
5
, which is (
4.42
), we obtain that for some
b>c^{\star}/2
it holds

\lim_{x_{1}\to+\infty}\lVert\mathfrak{U}(x_{1},\cdot)-\mathfrak{q}^{+}(\cdot+\tau^{+})\rVert_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}e^{bx_{1}}=0

(6.50)

for some
\tau^{+}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. This concludes the proof of the statement.

The proof of Theorem
1
is concluded.
∎

6.3
Proof of Theorem
2
completed

Assume that
(H6)
and
(H7)
hold. Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
1
, we have that the assumptions of Theorem
6
are fulfilled if we choose as in Lemma
6.1
. Therefore, Theorem
2
is readily obtained by a straightforward rewriting of Theorem
6
.
∎

6.4
Proof of Theorem
3

We now provide the proof of Theorem
3
, which is a consequence of the following results, which are more general as required by Theorem
3
and might be of independent interest:

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume that
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
hold. Let
(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})\in\Sigma^{2}
(possibly equal) and
q\in X(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})
. Assume moreover that there exists
L^{+}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
U\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}([L^{+},+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
uniformly continuous and such that

\int_{L^{+}}^{+\infty}\lvert E(U(x_{1},\cdot))-E(q)\rvert dx_{1}<+\infty,

(6.51)

\lim_{x_{1}\to+\infty}\lVert U(x_{1},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.52)

Then, it holds that

\lim_{x_{1}\to+\infty}\lVert U(x_{1},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0

(6.53)

and

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}([L^{+},+\infty),{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.54)

Similarly, we have the following

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume that
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
hold. Let
(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})\in\Sigma^{2}
(possibly equal) and
q\in X(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})
. Assume moreover that there exists
L^{-}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
U\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((-\infty,L^{-}]\times{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
uniformly continuous and such that

\int_{-\infty}^{L^{-}}lvertE(U(x_{1},\cdot))-E(q)\rvert dx_{1}<+\infty,

(6.55)

\lim_{x_{1}\to-\infty}\lVert U(x_{1},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.56)

Then, it holds that

\lim_{x_{1}\to-\infty}\lVert U(x_{1},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0

(6.57)

and

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}((-\infty,L^{-}],{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.58)

In the proof of Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
, we will need to use the following fact:

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume that
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
hold. Let
(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})\in\Sigma^{2}
(possibly equal) and
q\in X(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})
. Assume that
(q_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a sequence in
X(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert q_{n}-q\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0

(6.59)

and

\lim_{n\to\infty}E(q_{n})=E(q),

(6.60)

then, it holds that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert q_{n}-q\rVert_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.61)

Proof.

First, notice that

\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\lVert q_{n}\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}<+\infty.

(6.62)

Indeed, (
6.60
) implies that
(q_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is bounded in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
which, in combination with (
6.59
)) means that
(q_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is bounded in
H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
, hence in
L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. We also have that

\nabla V(q)\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k}),

(6.63)

which follows easily from the fact that
V
is smooth and quadratic near the wells. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we write the following expansion

V(q_{n})=V(q)+\langle\nabla V(q),q_{n}-q\rangle+\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}V(q+\lambda(q_{n}-q))(q_{n}-q)(q_{n}-q)d\lambda,

(6.64)

which holds pointwise in
{\mathbb{R}}
. Therefore, Cauchy-Schwartz inequality implies

\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\lvert V(q_{n})-V(q)\rvert\right)^{2}\leq\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\lvert\nabla V(q)\rvert^{2}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\\
\lvert u\rvert\leq\lVert q_{n}-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\end{subarray}}\lvert D^{2}V(u)\rvert\right)\lVert q_{n}-q\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}^{2},

(6.65)

hence, by (
6.62
) and (
6.63
) we find a constant
C>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\lvert V(q_{n})-V(q)\rvert\leq C\lVert q_{n}-q\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}

(6.66)

which by (
6.59
) means that
V(q_{n})-V(q)\to 0
in
L^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
. As a consequence, (
6.60
) implies that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert q_{n}^{\prime}\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=\lVert q^{\prime}\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}.

(6.67)

Suppose now by contradiction that (
6.61
) does not hold. Then, we can find a subsequence
(q_{n_{m}})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\hat{\delta}>0
such that for all
m\in{\mathbb{N}}

\lVert q_{n_{m}}-q\rVert_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\geq\hat{\delta}.

(6.68)

Since
(q_{n_{m}}^{\prime})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is bounded in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
, it converges weakly in
L^{2}
up to an extraction, and the limit is
q^{\prime}
by uniqueness of the limit in the sense of distributions. By (
6.67
), we have that such a subsequence also converges strongly in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
, which combining with (
6.59
) contradicts (
6.68
).
∎

We now prove Lemma
6.2
. The proof of Lemma
6.3
being analogous, we skip it.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Assume by contradiction that (
6.53
) does not hold. Then, we can find a sequence
(x_{1,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
[L^{+},+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}
such that
x_{1,n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
as well as
\hat{\delta}>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

\lVert U(x_{1,n},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\geq\hat{\delta}.

(6.69)

By uniform continuity, there exists
\nu>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
we have

\max_{x_{1}\in[x_{1,n}-\nu,x_{1,n}+\nu]}\lVert U(x_{1},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\geq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{2}

(6.70)

Let
A:=\cup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}[x_{1,n}-\nu,x_{1,n}+\nu]
. By (
6.51
) we have that

\int_{A}(E(U(x_{1},\cdot))-E(q))dx_{1}<+\infty,

(6.71)

and since
A
has positive measure and it is unbounded by above, we find a sequence
(y_{1,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
A
such that
y_{1,n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}E(U(y_{1,n},\cdot))=E(q)
. Combining this fact with (
6.52
), we have that assumptions (
6.59
) and (
6.60
) in Lemma
6.4
hold, which means that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert U(y_{1,n},\cdot)-q\rVert_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0

(6.72)

which contradicts (
6.70
). Therefore, we have shown that (
6.53
) holds. In order to prove (
6.54
), we first show that there exists
\underline{L}^{+}\leq L^{+}
such that

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}([\underline{L}^{+},+\infty),{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0.

(6.73)

We prove (
6.73
) by contradiction. The other case being handled in an analogous fashion, assume that there exists a sequence
(x_{2,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
{\mathbb{R}}
such that
x_{2,n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
, a sequence
(x_{1,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
[L^{+},+\infty)
tending to
+\infty
and
\hat{\delta}>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

\lvert U(x_{1,n},x_{2,n})-\hat{\sigma}_{+}\rvert\geq\hat{\delta}.

(6.74)

Since we already proved that (
6.53
) holds, there exists
N_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
n\geq{\mathbb{N}}
we have

\lVert U(x_{1,n},\cdot)-q\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}\leq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{4}

(6.75)

and, since
q\in X(\hat{\sigma}_{-},\hat{\sigma}_{+})
, there exists
\hat{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that for all
t\geq\hat{t}
we have

\lvert q(t)-\hat{\sigma}_{+}\rvert\leq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{4}.

(6.76)

Let
N_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
be such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x_{2,n}\geq\hat{t}
. Taking any
n\geq\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
, we obtain by (
6.75
) and (
6.76
) that

\lvert U(x_{1,n},x_{2,n})-\hat{\sigma}_{+}\rvert\leq\frac{\delta}{2},

(6.77)

which contradicts (
6.74
) and establishes (
6.73
). In order to establish (
6.54
), we handle the limit
x_{2}\to+\infty
, as the other one is treated identically. Let
\rho_{\Sigma}^{+}:=\mathrm{dist}(\sigma^{+},\Sigma\setminus\{\sigma^{+}\})>0
.
We claim that for every
\tilde{L}\geq L^{+}
we have that if

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}([\tilde{L},+\infty),{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0,

(6.78)

then

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}([\tilde{L}-\eta_{\Sigma}^{+},+\infty),{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0,

(6.79)

where

\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}:=\min\left\{\tilde{L}-L^{+},\frac{\rho_{\Sigma}}{4\lVert D\mathfrak{U}\rVert_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}\right\}.

(6.80)

Such a claim allows to easily complete the proof of (
6.54
) by a finite induction process, due to the fact that (
6.73
) holds.
∎

It remains to establish one claim in the proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Proof that (
6.78
) implies (
6.79
).

Assume that (
6.78
) holds. We show that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,\eta_{\Sigma}^{+})
we have

\lim_{x_{2}\to\pm\infty}\lVert U(\cdot,x_{2})-\hat{\sigma}_{\pm}\rVert_{L^{\infty}([\tilde{L}-\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}+\varepsilon,+\infty),{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}=0,

(6.81)

which clearly implies (
6.79
) by uniform continuity. Fix then
\varepsilon\in(0,\eta_{\Sigma}^{+})
. By assumption, there exists
\overline{x}_{2}^{+}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that for all
x_{2}\geq\overline{x}_{2}^{+}
we have

\lvert U(\tilde{L},x_{2})-\sigma^{+}\rvert\leq\frac{\rho_{\Sigma}^{+}}{4},

(6.82)

which, by (
6.80
) implies that for all
(x_{1},x_{2})\in[\tilde{L}-\eta^{+}_{\Sigma}+\varepsilon,\tilde{L}]\times[\overline{x}_{2},+\infty)
, it holds

\lvert U(x_{1},x_{2})-\sigma^{+}\rvert\leq\frac{\rho_{\Sigma}^{+}}{2}

(6.83)

and the definition of
\rho_{\Sigma}^{+}
gives in turn that for all such
(x_{1},x_{2})
and
\sigma\in\Sigma\setminus\{\sigma^{+}\}
we have

\lvert U(x_{1},x_{2})-\sigma\rvert\geq\frac{\rho_{\Sigma}^{+}}{2}.

(6.84)

Assume now that (
6.81
) does not hold. Then, inequalities (
6.83
) and (
6.84
) imply that we can find a sequence
(x_{1,n},x_{2,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
contained in
[\tilde{L}-\eta^{+}_{\Sigma}+\varepsilon,\tilde{L}]\times[\overline{x}_{2},+\infty)
, such that
x_{2,n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
and
\hat{\delta}>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma

\lvert U(x_{1,n},x_{2,n})-\sigma\rvert\geq\hat{\delta}.

(6.85)

By uniform continuity, we can find
\nu\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

(x_{1},x_{2})\in B((x_{1,n},x_{2,n}),\nu)\subset[\tilde{L}-\eta^{+}_{\Sigma},\tilde{L}]\times[\overline{x}_{2},+\infty)

(6.86)

we have for all
\sigma\in\Sigma

\lvert U(x_{1},x_{2})-\sigma\rvert\geq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{2}

(6.87)

or, equivalently

V(U(x_{1},x_{2}))\geq V_{\hat{\delta}/2}:=\min\left\{V(u):u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\mathrm{dist}(u,\Sigma)\geq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{2}\right\}

(6.88)

which is positive by
(H1)
and
(H3)
. Up to an extraction and since
x_{2,n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
, we can assume that whenever
n\not=m
we have

B((x_{1,n},x_{2,n}),\nu)\cap B((x_{1,m},x_{2,m}),\nu)=\emptyset,

(6.89)

which, due to the definition of
\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}
in (
6.80
) and (
6.88
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{L^{+}}^{+\infty}\lvert E(U(x_{1}))-E(q)\rvert dx_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\tilde{L}-\eta^{+}_{\Sigma}}^{\tilde{L}}E(U(x_{1}))dx_{1}-\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}E(q)

(6.90)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\left(\int_{B((x_{1,n},x_{2,n}),\nu)}V(U(x_{1},x_{2}))dx_{1}dx_{2}\right)-\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}E(q)

(6.91)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}(\pi\nu^{2}V_{\hat{\delta}/2})-\eta_{\Sigma}^{+}E(q)=+\infty,

(6.92)

which enters in contradiction with (
6.51
). Therefore, the claim has been proven.
∎

We have now all the necessary ingredients for completing the proof of Theorem
3
:

Proof of Theorem
3
completed.

Let
(c^{\star},\mathfrak{U})
be the solution given by Theorem
1
, interpreted via the choices made in Lemma
6.1
. We will invoke Lemma
6.2
. The
L^{2}
exponential convergence (
2.25
) given by Theorem
1
implies in particular that assumption (
6.52
) in Lemma
6.2
holds with
U=\mathfrak{U}
,
q=\mathfrak{q}^{+}(\cdot+\tau^{+})
. Moreover, since
E_{2,c^{\star}}(\mathfrak{U})=0<+\infty
, assumption (
6.51
) in Lemma
6.2
holds for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
in view of the definition of
E_{2,c^{\star}}
(recall that
c^{\star}>0
). Finally, we have by Theorem
1
that
\mathfrak{U}\in\mathcal{C}^{2,\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, so that
\mathfrak{U}
is uniformly continuous. As a consequence, Lemma
6.2
applies and we have (
6.53
) and (
6.54
) for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and this is exactly (
2.29
) and (
2.30
) for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Assume now that
(H7)
holds, so that Theorem
2
applies. We will show that we can invoke Lemma
6.3
. We have that (
2.28
) in Theorem
2
implies that (
6.56
) in Lemma
6.3
holds with
U=\mathfrak{U}
and
q=\mathfrak{q}^{-}(\cdot+\tau^{-})
. Moreover, the abstract result Proposition
5.4
in combination with Lemma
6.1
implies in particular that for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
, (
6.56
) in Lemma
6.3
holds. Since
\mathfrak{U}
is uniformly continuous, Lemma
6.3
applies, which means that (
6.57
) holds, so that we have proven (
2.31
) in Theorem
3
. Moreover, for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have that (
6.58
) holds, which combined with (
2.30
) (which also holds for all
L\in{\mathbb{R}}
) gives (
2.32
) and completes the proof.
∎

6.5
Proof of Theorem
4
completed

Assume that
(H6)
and
(H7)
hold. Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
1
, we have that the assumptions of Theorem
7
are fulfilled if we choose as in Lemma
6.1
. Notice that Theorem
4
is exactly Theorem
7
if we choose the abstract objects as in Lemma
6.1
. Therefore, Theorem
4
is established.
∎
